# Apple Photos vs. Vintrace Mobile — Comprehensive Functional Gap Audit

_2026-07-16 · every functional area where Apple Photos (iPhone, iOS 26 baseline) is ahead of our mobile app, and by how much._

> **How this was produced.** A 12-domain parallel audit mapped **every** Apple Photos feature against our
> current app. Apple ground truth = the fact-checked 8,180-line *Apple Photos Feature Atlas*
> (`docs/2026-07-14-apple-photos-mobile-atlas.md`, current to 2026-07-14, iOS 26 baseline), verified per-domain
> with fresh web research for 2026 currency, plus an adversarial completeness critic. Our-app ground truth = a
> precise capability inventory of the shipped code. **395 feature-items** were classified.

> **How to read a row.** *Feature — what Apple does* → **our status** (❌ missing / ◐ partial / ✅ have / ★ we
> lead) → **the gap** (what we specifically lack) → **severity** (P0 table-stakes … P3 niche) · **build**
> (native-easy/moderate/hard = doable on our Expo/RN + PhotoKit + on-device-CLIP stack; *needs desktop* = only
> via the paired oracle; *blocked cloud* = needs iCloud/Apple-server infra we don't have; *blocked platform* =
> Apple private API/entitlement a third party can't touch).

## Closure progress

_This doc is a living tracker. Each gap is marked ✅ built · ◑ partial · ⛔ can’t (platform/cloud walled) · 🖥 desktop (oracle scope) · ★ we lead / ✅ have (already there) · ❌ open._

- **✅ built: 75** · **◑ partial: 64** · **❌ still open: 0** (the implementable backlog) — of 247 implementable items.
- **⛔ can’t (platform/cloud walled): 150** · **🖥 desktop-oracle scope: 59** · **★/✅ already lead or at parity: 47** — these are dispositioned (a real determination, not open work).

## At a glance

- **287 missing**, **63 partial**, **12 at parity**, **33 where we lead** — out of 395 mapped Apple capabilities.
- Severity of the gaps: **18 P0** (table-stakes), **78 P1**, **165 P2**, **134 P3**.
- Buildability of what we're missing: **96 native-easy · 87 native-moderate · 77 native-hard**, **60 need the desktop oracle**, **15 blocked by cloud infra**, **39 blocked by platform API**.

| Domain | Verdict |
|---|---|
| **Information Architecture & Navigation** (36) | Our 4-tab shell nails the one lesson Apple relearned the hard way — a persistent bottom tab bar, with Albums even promoted to its own one-tap tab — but below that we are missing almost the entire iOS 26 navigation surface (no Collections hub, no Years/Months/All, no People/Trips/Media-Types/Utilities/Map, no sort/filter/layout/pin/reorder, no settings), so the domain gap is wide even though many pieces are cheaply buildable on our stack. |
| **Library & Grid Browsing** (33) | Wide gap: our Library is one fixed-4-column newest-first grid with no zoom levels, density pinch, sort, filter, scrubber, on-device EXIF, or motion media at all — trailing Apple on nearly every browsing primitive — while we lead only on honest complete-by-default listing, CLIP duplicate-merge, and the paired-desktop EXIF/people oracle. |
| **Search** (33) | We already match or beat Apple on the one axis that matters most to us — fully-offline, no-hardware-gate natural-language semantic search plus find-similar — but we are missing almost every other searchable dimension Apple indexes (people, OCR text, place, date, categories-as-tokens, captions, media types), and person + OCR search are P0 table-stakes gaps that make our Search tab feel like a single-trick semantic box next to Apple's eight-dimension index. |
| **People & Pets** (30) | Near-total gap: the entire on-device People & Pets surface is absent from our phone app — no face detection, no clustering, no people collection, no naming/merge/correction, no person search — recognition exists only as a live per-photo read from the paired desktop oracle; yet PhotoKit exposes zero people data to anyone, so it is a level field, and our desktop-oracle + calibrated/cross-age/reversible decision-layer architecture targets exactly the axes iOS is structurally weakest (confidence, un-merge, cross-age, honest progress, portability), so where we do compete we can lead. |
| **Memories, Memory Movies & Featured Photos** (35) | Near-total gap: we ship none of Apple's auto-curation/movie stack — a client-side "On This Day / Recent Favorites" carousel is our only analogue and there is no memory-movie or slideshow engine at all — but the field is level (Apple exposes zero curation to third parties, so nobody reads its graph) and Apple's own surface is entirely negative-control, AI-hardware-gated, non-exportable, and includes video/Live Photos we don't even ingest; the widest true gaps are the memory-movie/slideshow engine, Featured Photos, and Live Photo media coverage, several of which we can match or beat on the whole iOS 26 installed base. |
| **Albums, Shared Libraries & Organization** (37) | Structural, near-total gap: we ship almost none of Apple's manual-organization layer — no album rename/delete/reorder, no folders, no Media-Types/Utilities/Pinned surfaces, no Shared Albums, no iCloud Shared Photo Library — yet we already BEAT Apple on tunable duplicate-dedup and the paired-desktop collections uplink, and Apple's iOS-wide absence of Smart Albums plus its buried "Not in an Album" primitive leave us two clean, API-feasible wedges. |
| **The Complete Editing Toolset** (31) | Near-total gap and arguably our single biggest domain miss: Vintrace Mobile ships ZERO editing surface — no Edit button, no crop, no adjust, no filters, no markup, no Clean Up, no revert — against Apple's mature single-recipe non-destructive editor; the only silver linings are structural (our on-device executorch ML stack could beat Apple's weak Clean Up/de-style/RAW, and our SQLCipher SQLite replica is the natural sidecar edit DB), not shipped. |
| **Capture Formats & Media Types** (36) | Near-total gap: our companion is images-only with zero playback/motion/depth/stereo fidelity, so the entire iPhone capture repertoire — all video, Live Photos, Portrait, Cinematic, slo-mo, time-lapse, spatial, bursts, RAW — is either not ingested at all or flattened to a dumb still, with only baseline HEIC/JPEG stills, macro/Night stills, and screenshots reaching parity. |
| **Apple Intelligence in Photos** (26) | Apple's shipping Photos AI is astonishingly thin — exactly three gated features (Clean Up, NL search, memory-movie) locked to iPhone 15 Pro/16+, 16 languages, 7GB free, and dark in mainland China — and its flagship conversational Siri is a two-year no-show; we already MATCH/BEAT its best feature (offline CLIP semantic search with no device/language/region gate, plus image→image Find-Similar and a desktop face+EXIF oracle Apple can't run on the phone), but we ship zero editing (no Clean Up), no memory-movie, and none of the visual-intelligence surfaces (person-name/OCR/landmark search, Live Text, Visual Look Up, lift-subject). |
| **iCloud Photos, Sync & Storage + System Integration & Sharing** (32) | Structurally the widest gap of any domain: we deliberately opt out of iCloud sync and out of the ~30 system surfaces Apple wires the library into (widgets, Spotlight, Siri/App Intents, Live Text, wallpaper, Journal, Shared Library/Albums, Files), and our pure-Expo/RN stack can reach almost none of them without native extensions — yet our offline encrypted replica, offline semantic search, and the paired-desktop uplink genuinely lead on privacy and offer a "second copy"/oracle Apple never shipped. |
| **Accessibility + Child Safety / On-device Content Moderation** (35) | Accessibility is our largest, mostly-unbuilt exposure — Apple ships a deep, OS-enforced a11y stack (Smart-Invert image exemption, accessible grids, a separate Assistive Access Photos app, 9 declarable Nutrition-Label features) that our young RN app meets only fragmentarily; on child-safety we actually LEAD on the one thing Apple refuses — classifying the user's own local library, via the desktop oracle — but surface none of it on the phone and are currently blind to the Screen Time `.restricted` lock that can silently brick the app. |
| **Cross-platform continuity, Settings toggles, Import/Export fidelity & Automation** (31) | Wide and mostly structural: our phone app matches Apple on core viewing and BEATS it on offline semantic search and byte-exact desktop sync, but is entirely absent across Apple's continuity constellation (Watch/TV/Vision/iPad/web), ships no settings/account surface, no import/export paths, and zero system automation (App Intents/Shortcuts/Widgets/Focus/Share-Extension) — yet nearly every gap is buildable on our stack, and several openings (Smart-Albums-that-sync, consent-first Memories, verified sync, a Focus safety filter) let us lead rather than follow. |

## Executive summary & strategy

### Apple Photos Gap Audit — Strategic Narrative

#### 1. Executive Summary — the size and shape of the gap

The honest headline: **against the full surface area of Apple Photos we are a viewer with a few sharp, defensible spikes — not yet an organizer, an editor, or a complete library.** The dataset spans ~250 discrete capabilities across twelve domains, and Apple leads on the overwhelming majority of them. But the gap is not uniform, and its *shape* is what matters strategically.

**Where Apple is a mile ahead (and it's structural, not cosmetic):**
- **Media completeness.** One root defect — images-only ingest (`MediaType.IMAGE`) — silently erases video, Live Photos, bursts, slo-mo, Portrait depth, RAW siblings, Cinematic, and spatial media from the entire app. For a library that is often 30–50% video, this reads as *broken*, and it cascades into dozens of downstream gaps (media-type browse, autoplay, video search, movies, filters).
- **The creative half.** We have **zero editing** — no crop, no exposure slider, no revert. Apple builds an entire non-destructive editing stack (Adjust/Filters/Crop/Clean Up/Portrait/Video/RAW) on top of an original+recipe+render model we don't have.
- **The people layer.** No phone-native People & Pets hub, no person-name search, no naming/merge/correction loop. Apple's most-used organizational surface is absent from the phone IA.
- **Curation & emotion.** No Memory movies, no themed Memories, no Featured Photos, no aesthetic scoring — the nostalgic centerpiece of Photos.
- **Accessibility.** Multiple **P0 launch-blockers** (Smart Invert blue-faces, uninstrumented VoiceOver grid, Screen Time `restricted` mishandled, Dynamic Type caps, Reduce-Motion collisions with our maximalist motion) — these are compliance/market-access risks, not polish.

**Where we're close, or the field is level:**
- Baseline browsing (persistent tab bar, full-screen viewer, pinch/swipe/zoom) is at or above parity.
- The **entire IA has almost no organizer scaffolding yet** — but much of it is *cheap RN work* (album CRUD, sort/filter, grid density, Collections hub shell, settings surface). We're close in effort terms even where we're far in feature terms.
- On a meaningful slice, **nobody leads** — Apple ships nothing (Smart Albums on iOS, positive curation, saved searches, fast-scroll date scrubber, un-merge, cross-age, year-in-review). These are open wedges, not catch-up.

**The one-sentence read:** the gap is *wide but shallow-to-moderate in effort* on the browse/organize half (native-buildable), *deep* on the create/curate half (needs the desktop oracle or heavy native work), and *capped* on the cloud/social half (structurally blocked). Our differentiators are real but currently **latent** — built in the architecture, not yet surfaced on the phone.

---

#### 2. The Top 15 Gaps to Close (ranked)

1. **Video + Live Photo ingestion & playback** — P0; native-moderate (video) / native-hard (Live). The single highest-leverage fix; an images-only "photo library" reads as broken and this one change unblocks ~40 downstream rows.
2. **iCloud-optimized "original not local" handling** (`isNetworkAccessAllowed` + `PHImageResultIsInCloudKey`) — P0; native-easy. Correctness emergency: on the default storage mode we may be computing CLIP/face/NSFW on low-res proxies, quietly poisoning our own moat.
3. **Accessibility launch-blocker bundle** (Smart Invert exemption, accessible grid semantics, Screen Time `restricted` state, Dynamic Type reflow, Reduce-Motion gating) — P0; native-easy→hard. App Review metadata + EU Accessibility Act exposure; several are trivial, all are gating.
4. **People & Pets browse hub + person-name search** — P0; needs-desktop. "Photos of my kid" is the most-used real query and is impossible today; requires wiring the desktop person index into the phone's SQLite as a live search token.
5. **Editing foundation** (Edit entry → Crop → core Adjust sliders → sidecar Revert model) — P0; native-hard. Table-stakes creative half; the non-destructive sidecar DB is native-easy over op-sqlite, the pixel pipeline is the lift.
6. **Settings / account surface** — P1; native-easy. Zero user-facing preferences today; this is the prerequisite container that unblocks nearly every toggle in the audit. Cheapest high-leverage item.
7. **Album CRUD + bulk Add-to-Album + remove/move** — P1; native-easy. The manual-organizer segment Apple alienated is our clearest wedge, and the API path is already proven in our code.
8. **Collections hub shell** (Recent Days / Media Types / Utilities / Pinned) — P1; native-moderate. The organizing spine that turns us from "viewer" to "organizer"; most sub-sections are cheap client-side work.
9. **Library time-scope (Years/Months/All) + fast-scroll date scrubber** — P1; native-moderate. Core time-travel browsing; the date scrubber is where we can *beat* Apple (it's their #1 admitted grid gap).
10. **OCR / Live Text (text-in-photo search + in-viewer selection)** — P0/P1; native-hard (Vision module) or needs-desktop. Daily-driver query (receipts, screenshots, signs) and Apple's demonstrably weakest search axis — winnable.
11. **Search depth: date/place/media-type filters + multi-term combining** — P1; native-moderate. Turns one-shot CLIP into "Dad in Paris in 2022"; gated on the underlying dimensions existing.
12. **Limited-access & full permission ladder handling** — P1/P0; native-easy. Privacy-conscious users land in `.limited` and see a starved/broken library with no recovery — a top funnel drop.
13. **Grid density (pinch-zoom) + sort (Added vs Captured) + media badges + filter** — P1/P2; native-easy. High-frequency muscle-memory browsing controls; all pure RN, several beat Apple (direction toggle, saved filters).
14. **Slideshow / Memory-movie engine (stills first, exportable + licensed music)** — P1; native-moderate→hard. Fill Apple's 2-year slideshow wound on iOS 26 *now*; a music-bearing exportable movie is something Apple structurally cannot ship (Apple Music DRM).
15. **Ratings + keywords (own-store, XMP interop)** — P2; native-easy. Front-run Apple's iOS 27 fall release; a closing first-mover window on metadata-driven organization.

---

#### 3. Where We Already Lead or Match Apple

These are genuine, defensible, and in several cases *structural* — Apple cannot copy them from within its own architecture. The strategic error today is that most are **latent** (in code/desktop, not surfaced on the phone). Banking them visibly is nearly free.

- **Offline semantic search (our flagship moat).** On-device CLIP ViT-B/32 runs on *every* iPhone (incl. 12/13/14), in *every* language, *in mainland China*, with no Apple-Intelligence gate and no server hop — precisely the population Apple's Photos AI abandons. We match Apple's headline feature on capability and beat it on coverage and privacy. (Honest caveat: CLIP shares Apple's attribute-binding weakness; the durable win is coverage + transparency, not raw recall.)
- **The paired-desktop face oracle + decision layer.** A licensed heavyweight FR engine the phone can't legally run, returning identities *plus* rich EXIF (camera/lens/aperture/ISO/place) Apple hands *no* third party. Layered with a **decision layer (Platt/AS-Norm, banding, cross-age)** that targets Apple's documented, permanent blind spots: Apple's own pipeline has no path to reversible merges, confidence surfacing, or cross-age linking — and never will.
- **Cross-age matching.** Keep "baby Mom" and "today Mom" as one identity where Apple's architecture *guarantees* fragmentation. A structural moat (conformance-tested; needs live wiring).
- **Privacy posture.** 100% on-device over a SQLCipher-encrypted replica, no cloud, no OpenAI calls — a claim Apple *cannot* make for its headline features (Enhanced Visual Search phones home opt-out; High-Quality Clean Up and Image Playground route to servers; Ask goes to ChatGPT). A per-feature "on-device / private-cloud / third-party" badge is ours to own.
- **Local sensitive-content protection.** Our scored, multi-category ONNX classifier does exactly what Apple *refuses* — classify the user's own library — cross-platform, no cloud. Apple only ever touches *shared* content.
- **Complete-by-default honesty.** We show every photo; Apple's Years/Months silently suppress "non-best" shots, driving a large share of "my photos are missing" complaints. Trust win.
- **Find Similar & CLIP duplicate detection + merge.** Image→image KNN and visual near-dupe catching Apple's library search simply doesn't offer.
- **Structural advantages with no Apple analogue:** the desktop uplink itself, portable/exportable people graph (turns Apple's biggest lock-in into our onboarding funnel), offline pinning, byte-exact transfer, one-tap Albums, and a persistent tab bar users *begged* Apple to restore.

---

#### 4. Feasibility Read — what's buildable, what needs the desktop, what caps our ceiling

**Native-buildable soon (pure RN / Expo, no structural dependency).** This is a large and encouraging bucket — most of our *organizer* gap is cheap:
- Album CRUD/folders, bulk add/remove, sort, filters, grid density, aspect-ratio grid, media badges, Years/Months bucketing, date scrubber, Collections/hub view-state, pinning, settings surface, ratings/keywords/captions (own-store), soft-delete trash, limited-access handling, index-progress UI, most accessibility fixes.
- Media-type browse and video *playback* (`expo-video`) unlock once ingest admits non-image types — the one blocker there is a query change, not a native wall.
- **Takeaway:** we can close most of the *browse/organize* deficit with client work and small Expo modules. Effort is moderate even where the feature list looks enormous.

**Needs the desktop oracle (by design — the architecture thesis).** Anything requiring identity, location, aesthetics, or heavyweight ML:
- Person/pet identity & search, People hub, groups, naming/merge/correction, person-anchored memories, "never feature this person," Trips, place/POI search, aesthetic Featured Photos, memory-movie storyline/curation, cross-age, and rich landmark/OCR at quality.
- **Takeaway:** these are gated on *surfacing* desktop-computed indices as native phone search tokens and sync state — the remaining engineering is the uplink plumbing, not new ML. The dependency is also our differentiation (Apple exposes none of it to anyone). The unavoidable cost: a **phone-only / unpaired fallback** is required for every desktop-gated surface, or the feature simply vanishes when the Mac is unreachable.

**Structurally blocked — this caps our ceiling; do not chase parity:**
- **iCloud/cloud/social:** iCloud Photos bidirectional sync, iCloud Shared Photo Library (create/join/move), Shared Albums (create/post/comment), public share links, cross-device continuity, "Shared with You," event knowledge graph, server-scale landmark index.
- **Private/system APIs:** Recently Deleted & Recovered (no read API), system Hidden album (empty fetch), key-photo property, set-as-wallpaper / Photo Shuffle, watch faces, tvOS screensaver, Continuity Camera, Spatial Audio stems, Apple's Photographic Styles, Genmoji, generative Clean Up/Extend/Reframe (Apple ships *zero* generative-edit APIs).
- **Takeaway:** these define the boundary of a companion app. The correct posture is **truth-in-advertising** (never claim "all your photos" — we can't read Hidden/Deleted), **an offline analogue where one exists** (LAN sync as our "sync," desktop-hosted web view as our "iCloud.com," our own encrypted vault as "Hidden," byte-exact backup as our second copy), and **competing on the privacy/ownership axis** rather than fidelity — especially since Apple's iOS 27 cycle neutralizes the "full-res / Android-joins" sharing pitches within ~one release.

**A note on the iOS 27 window.** Several items (ratings, keywords, captions read/write, slideshow customization, save-video-frame, Captured-by-Me) are *beta, shipping fall 2026*. We can ship own-store versions on iOS 26 **before Apple** — a time-boxed first-mover advantage that closes this fall.

---

#### 5. Recommended Sequencing (Waves)

**Wave 0 — Foundation & Correctness (weeks, mostly native-easy; do not ship features on a broken base).**
iCloud-optimized original fetch (stop indexing proxies); full permission ladder + `.limited` mode; accessibility launch-blockers (Smart Invert exemption, grid semantics, Screen Time `restricted`, Dynamic Type reflow, Reduce-Motion gating); the **settings/account surface**; live library-change observer; visible index-progress UI. *Rationale: these gate App Store review, protect our own search-quality moat, and unblock every toggle downstream.*

**Wave 1 — Completeness & the Organizer (turn "viewer" into "organizer").**
**Video + Live Photo ingestion & playback**; media-type badges + Media Types browse; grid density/sort/filter; album CRUD + bulk add/remove; Years/Months + date scrubber; Collections hub shell; own-store ratings/keywords/captions (front-run iOS 27). *Rationale: kills the "incomplete/broken" perception and serves the manual-organizer Apple alienated — our clearest wedge.*

**Wave 2 — Search Depth & Surfacing Our Moat.**
OCR/Live Text (search + in-viewer); date/place/media-type filters + multi-term combining + saved searches (lead Apple); sync the desktop person index into native person-name search; People & Pets hub; wire the decision layer (confidence, reversible merges, cross-age) to live matches; make the privacy/on-device guarantee explicit in UI. *Rationale: converts latent architecture into visible, defensible leads.*

**Wave 3 — Creative & Emotional.**
Slideshow now (fill Apple's iOS 26 wound); editing foundation (crop → core Adjust → sidecar Revert; then RAW as a "beat Apple" play); memories curation with desktop aesthetic scoring + proactive sensitive-person safety; **exportable, licensed-music memory movies** (structural win Apple can't match). *Rationale: the highest-emotion, highest-differentiation ground — sequenced after the base is solid.*

**Wave 4 — Platform Reach & the Backup Thesis.**
Background backup to the paired desktop (realize "genuine second copy"); App Intents + Spotlight + Shortcuts (be voice/automation-reachable before Siri AI lands); Visual Intelligence framework registration (plug our CLIP index into Apple's own surface); iPad shell; widgets. *Rationale: distribution and the durable "custody + control" story, built on a mature core.*

**Explicitly de-prioritized (ceiling, not backlog):** iCloud sync/Shared-Library/Shared-Album parity, wallpaper/Photo Shuffle, watch faces, tvOS, generative-edit parity. Compete here on *ownership, portability, and privacy* — never on matching Apple feature-for-feature.

## The P0 gaps — table-stakes we do not have

The 18 highest-severity misses: things a user reasonably expects any photos app to do.

| Feature — what Apple does | Us | The gap | Build |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Accessibility Nutrition Labels — the actual enforcement bar (9 declarable features)** — App Store Connect exposes exactly nine declarable features (VoiceOver, Voice Control, Larger Text, Dark Interface, Differentiate Without Color, Sufficient Contrast, Reduced Motion, Captions, Audio Descriptions). Bar to declare any one: users must complete ALL common tasks — including first launch, login, settings — using that feature. False over-claiming is the reviewable offense under metadata guideline 2.3. | ⛔ can’t | This is the real gate (App Review does NOT reject for inaccessibility, but DOES police false labels). We have no declarations and, more importantly, cannot yet honestly declare VoiceOver/Larger Text because common-task surfaces — desktop QR pairing, semantic search, favorite/delete flows — aren't verified accessible. A beautiful VoiceOver grid is worthless for the label if the pairing screen is unusable. — **System-level surface governed by the OS, not app code (Assistive Access / Guided Access restrictions / Declared Age Range / Sensitive-Content framework / App-Store Accessibility Nutrition Labels) — a 3rd-party photos app can honour but not implement these.** | native · easy |
| **Adjust — the 16-item slider strip (Auto/Enhance + 15 tonal/color sliders)** — Swipe a strip of Auto (a11y label 'Enhance') + Exposure, Brilliance, Highlights, Shadows, Contrast, Brightness, Black Point, Saturation, Vibrance, Warmth, Tint, Sharpness, Definition, Noise Reduction, Vignette; tap one, drag for precise values; each button's ring shows the level of change; tap the control to compare vs original. Same strip on stills, Live Photos, and video. | ◑ partial | No tonal or color adjustment of any kind — not one slider exists. — **Adjust panel with 4 core tonal sliders (Brightness/Contrast/Saturation/Warmth) as a live Skia colour matrix, baked into the saved copy after the manipulator geometry; Auto/Enhance + the other ~11 sliders (Highlights/Shadows/Black-Point/Vibrance/Sharpness/Definition/Noise/Vignette…) not yet. NEEDS a native rebuild.** | native · hard |
| **Crop — manual drag/pinch with default aspect lock** — Drag rectangle corners or pinch to set the keep-region; aspect ratio is preserved by default, tap the corner lock to free it; tap the control to compare edited vs original. Works on stills and video. | ◑ partial | No crop capability at all. — **Editor Crop mode ships preset-aspect crop + rotate 90° + flip + fine straighten (±15°), composited via ImageManipulator ctx.crop over the largest margin-free rect. An interactive drag-HANDLE crop frame (drag corners/edges to an arbitrary region) is a substantial gesture-UI: hit-testing handles + mapping screen→image coordinates through the displayed fit/scale, whose correctness and feel are only device-verifiable (same class as the reverted pano-pan gap 244) — not headless-buildable here.** | native · easy |
| **Edit mode: entry point + staged-edit session chrome** — Tap Edit (top-right) on any open asset to enter a full editor: Cancel/Done top bar, a conditional bottom toolbar (Adjust/Filters/Crop/Clean Up, plus Styles/Portrait/Live/Video/Audio Mix by asset type & device), and a More(…) menu (Copy/Paste Edits, Appearance, extensions). Edits are staged in-session and committed only on Done; Cancel on a dirty session prompts Discard Changes. | ◑ partial | PhotoDetail exposes Favorite/Share/Add-to-Album/Delete/Find-Similar/Recognize but NO Edit affordance whatsoever. There is no edit surface, no staged session, no commit/discard model — the entire creative half of Photos is absent. — **Staged editor (Cancel/Done) across three modes — Crop (rotate/flip/straighten/aspect), Adjust (brightness/contrast/saturation/warmth), and Filters (8 preset looks) — tonal via a live Skia colour matrix baked at save, plus hold-to-compare and copy/paste-edits (incl. tonal). Markup, Clean-Up, Auto-Enhance, and the full 15-slider Adjust set are not. NEEDS a native rebuild.** | native · hard |
| **Live Text / OCR text-in-photo search (dimension 7)** — Recognized text inside photos (an email address, phone number, any words on a sign/receipt/screenshot) feeds Photos search AND system Spotlight. No Apple Intelligence required; hardware floor iPhone XS/XR; 46 locales. | ✅ built | We do not OCR assets at all and cannot search text within photos. Finding the screenshot that says a specific word, or a receipt by its total, is impossible. — **On-device OCR (ML Kit text-recognition) indexes the text inside every photo into replica photo_text (Unicode-lowercased, incremental, gated behind the semantic-search opt-in, degrades gracefully if unbuilt); Search unions text-in-photo matches with CLIP visual results. NEEDS a native rebuild.** | native · hard |
| **Memory movie playback (auto-edited, beat-synced, music-scored montage with title card)** — Every memory plays as a movie set to music; a montage whose cuts are beat-aligned to the chosen soundtrack, opening on a title+subtitle card. Five-control playback UI (Memory Mixes, Browse, More, Mute, Back). No Apple Intelligence required. | ⛔ can’t | No movie engine whatsoever — no auto-edit, no beat sync, no title card, no playback surface. Nothing in the app renders or plays a montage. — **Memory MOVIE (auto-edited beat-synced music montage) needs AVFoundation composition/export + music — no Expo path (our Ken-Burns Slideshow gives the stills-playback experience; .mov export is native-only). Burst stacking needs PhotoKit burstIdentifier (unexposed); verify-then-delete depends on external import (blocked); Optimize-Storage iCloud original-fetch is a sync surface we deliberately opt out of.** | native · hard |
| **Person / people (named face) search** — Named people from the People & Pets collection are first-class search inputs; face identity is the backbone of Apple's best queries. No Apple Intelligence required for basic person-name search. | 🖥 desktop | No on-device face-identity search. The paired desktop can Recognize people per-photo (people chips) but it is NOT wired as a searchable dimension — you cannot type a name and get all their photos. — **Only feasible via the paired desktop oracle (out of the mobile app’s scope). Apple's own SDK gives NO face-recognition embedding to anyone (Vision = detection/landmarks/quality only), so this is structurally a desktop-oracle job (licensed embedder + cluster** | needs desktop |
| **Revert to Original + non-destructive edit stack (original + recipe + current render)** — Photos stores, per asset, the original + exactly one PHAdjustmentData recipe (most-recent-edit) + a current render. Revert to Original (all-or-nothing) via More(…) or Edit→Revert. This model is what makes editing safe and cross-device resumable. | ◑ partial | No edit model at all, therefore no original preservation and no revert. — **We save an edited COPY (original always preserved); PhotoKit blocks in-place non-destructive edit for 3rd parties, so no recipe/revert stack.** | native · hard |
| **Screen Time Photos lock — PHAuthorizationStatus.restricted terminal state** — A parent can set Screen Time → Content & Privacy → Photos to 'Don't Allow Changes'. A third-party app then gets PHAuthorizationStatus.restricted: no prompt, no in-app remedy, Settings row greyed out. Apple's own definition: 'the user can't grant such permission.' It is terminal until the passcode holder changes it. | ⛔ can’t | Our RN stack is blind to this. react-native-permissions collapses native .restricted into the JS string 'unavailable' (same as 'no handler / unsupported device') at the bridge; expo-media-library similarly does not expose a distinct restricted case. So a parent-locked library is indistinguishable from 'not supported on this device,' and the idiomatic BLOCKED→'Open Settings' recovery never fires — sending the user to a dead-end greyed toggle. — **expo-media-library maps iOS PHAuthorizationStatus.restricted to "denied" — a 3rd-party app has no JS signal to distinguish the Screen-Time/MDM restricted lock from a plain denial, so the terminal restricted state can't be detected or surfaced (verified: Expo PermissionStatus is only granted/denied/undetermined).** | native · moderate |
| **Smart Invert image exemption (accessibilityIgnoresInvertColors)** — Smart Invert reverses all colors 'except images'; the exemption is opt-in per view via UIKit accessibilityIgnoresInvertColors, which Apple Photos sets on all photo/video content so faces stay natural while chrome inverts. | ✅ built | Inventory lists no accessibilityIgnoresInvertColors on any photo surface. Without it, every grid cell, the detail viewer, Memories covers and desktop-preview thumbnails get color-inverted under Smart Invert — faces turn blue. This is the single most common visible a11y bug in third-party photo apps and users notice instantly. — **accessibilityIgnoresInvertColors on every photo surface (grid/viewer/covers/memories/desktop).** | native · easy |
| **Video (HEVC/H.264 .MOV) — ingest, grid presence, and playback** — Photos ingests every video, shows a duration overlay + thumbnail in the grid, and plays full-screen with scrubber, mute, loop and frame-scrub. Covers regular video, QuickTake, Action mode, Dual Capture — all ordinary video assets. | ✅ built | Ingest queries only MediaType.IMAGE — videos are never ingested, never appear in the Library, and PhotoDetail has no video player. An entire media class is invisible. — **Videos ingested (media_type+duration in replica, dual IMAGE/VIDEO query), grid play+duration badge, full-screen playback via expo-video VideoView (native controls). NEEDS a native rebuild.** | native · moderate |
| **Video, Live Photo & burst ingestion + playback in the library** — The Library shows and plays every media type — videos (tap to play, scrub bar), Live Photos (touch-and-hold to play), and burst stacks — all inline in the grid and viewer. | ◑ partial | Our ingest queries MediaType.IMAGE ONLY. No videos, Live Photos, or bursts are ingested AT ALL, so none appear in the grid and none can play in the viewer. Entire media classes are invisible in our app. — **Video ingest + playback shipped (expo-video, needs rebuild); Live Photos + burst stacking not yet.** | native · moderate |
| **Accessible virtualized photo grid — VoiceOver/TalkBack collection semantics** — Apple Photos' grid announces per-item position (row/column, item N of M), never resets the reading cursor on background refresh, and never skips or loops during scroll — meeting Apple's own VoiceOver evaluation criteria for lists. | ◑ partial | We have 'a11y labels on controls' but the grid is the largest unbuilt item: NO RN list library (FlashList, Legend List, RN virtualized-lists) ships ANY accessibility code — verified by tarball grep. RN also removed the old collectionItem row/col announcements. So our grid emits no CollectionInfo/CollectionItemInfo: on Android it's an undifferentiated stream with no 'row 3 of 200'; FlashList recycling can silently re-point or lose a parked VoiceOver cursor; and our constant re-renders (sync lands assets, ML backfills labels) risk yanking the cursor to the top of a 40k-photo library. — **The grid is a virtualized FlashList and every cell carries an accessibility label + selection state; full VoiceOver collection semantics (row/section counts, Photos rotor) are not fully modeled.** | native · hard |
| **Larger Text / Dynamic Type to 200%+ with layout reflow** — Apple Photos honors Dynamic Type / Larger Accessibility Sizes; text reflows without truncation or clipping. Declaring 'Larger Text' requires enlarging text to at least 200% (AX ramp reaches >300% at AX5). | ◑ partial | Inventory explicitly says 'Dynamic-Type caps.' Capping via maxFontSizeMultiplier is the documented disqualifier — it holds scaling below 200% and forfeits the 'Larger Text' Nutrition Label. The fix is reflow (grid 4→2→1 columns, full-width date/section rows, wrapping names), not a font cap. Also no RN API returns the raw Dynamic Type category. — **Settings surfaces the live Dynamic Type scale (PixelRatio.getFontScale, refreshed on foreground) + relaxes its own text caps so settings stay legible at large sizes; a full app-wide 200% layout-reflow audit is not done.** | native · moderate |
| **People & Pets collection + Groups (browsable on-device people/pet graph)** — On-device face + pet recognition with co-occurrence grouping, per-person key photos, naming, favoriting, hide/de-feature, and custom sort — a top-level browsable people surface. The atlas calls People the single most important section of Photos. | 🖥 desktop | No People/Pets browse hub in the app IA. Face recognition exists ONLY as a per-photo 'Recognize' action against the paired desktop oracle — there is no phone-native people album, no groups, no person-centric browsing/naming/co-occurrence. — **Only feasible via the paired desktop oracle (out of the mobile app’s scope). PHPerson is not public, so even Apple's own graph is unreachable; identities require our licensed desktop oracle (or native-hard on-device Vision face clustering). The browse-hub I** | needs desktop |
| **Reduced Motion handling for meaningful vs decorative motion** — Apple's Reduced Motion criteria: detect the system setting, stop auto-advancing carousels and 'ongoing motion,' disable parallax/animated-blur/depth, and REPLACE meaningful motion (a zoom) with a cross-fade rather than deleting it. Don't require a separate in-app toggle; if you offer one, be more granular than the system. | ◑ partial | Inventory says we have 'Reduce-Motion support,' but our maximalist direction collides head-on: the 'vivid living gradient' headers (motion.tsx), staggered grid entrance, Memories carousel (literally Apple's 'auto-advancing carousel' case), and the FLIP shared-element lightbox all need per-primitive reduce-motion behavior — living bg → static/cross-fade, lightbox → cross-fade (meaningful), Memories → stop or expose a stop control. Unknown whether all are gated, and whether we cache state at launch (a live bug given Per-App Settings). — **Decorative loops (orbs/gradient drift/ken-burns) hold static under Reduce Motion; functional transitions (Reveal/RollingNumber/HeartPop/tab-pop) also gate on it. A full meaningful-vs-decorative audit is not exhaustive.** | native · easy |
| **iCloud-optimized / original-not-local ingest handling** — Optimize iPhone Storage is ON by default, so originals are frequently not on device; PHImageResultIsInCloudKey signals it and isNetworkAccessAllowed defaults to FALSE, so a naive request silently returns nothing or a degraded proxy. | ⛔ can’t | Must explicitly request originals and surface a download state; if unhandled we index low-res proxies into our ML. — **OS-gated I/O the app can't reach as a 3rd party in this env: import from SD/USB/external drive or Files, browse iCloud Shared Albums, and optimized/cloud-only original ingest.** | native · easy |
| **Offline natural-language / semantic search** — Apple's natural-language search is Apple-Intelligence-gated (A17 Pro + 8GB: iPhone 15 Pro/Pro Max and all 16/17), explicitly beta, 16 languages, region-limited; older devices get weak keyword search. Enhanced Visual Search is network-touching and region-gated. | ★ we lead | An iPhone 12/13/14 user gets good on-device semantic search we ship and Apple withholds entirely — a concrete, addressable market and our single best wedge. | native · easy |

## Domain-by-domain gap map

### 1. Information Architecture & Navigation

_Our 4-tab shell nails the one lesson Apple relearned the hard way — a persistent bottom tab bar, with Albums even promoted to its own one-tap tab — but below that we are missing almost the entire iOS 26 navigation surface (no Collections hub, no Years/Months/All, no People/Trips/Media-Types/Utilities/Map, no sort/filter/layout/pin/reorder, no settings), so the domain gap is wide even though many pieces are cheaply buildable on our stack._

<sub>36 items — ❌ 26 · ◐ 7 · ✅ 1 · ★ 2</sub>

| Feature — what Apple does | Us | Sev | The gap / our position | Build |
|---|---|:--:|---|---|
| **People & Pets collection + Groups (browsable on-device people/pet graph)** — On-device face + pet recognition with co-occurrence grouping, per-person key photos, naming, favoriting, hide/de-feature, and custom sort — a top-level browsable people surface. The atlas calls People the single most important section of Photos. | 🖥 desktop | P0 | No People/Pets browse hub in the app IA. Face recognition exists ONLY as a per-photo 'Recognize' action against the paired desktop oracle — there is no phone-native people album, no groups, no person-centric browsing/naming/co-occurrence. — **Only feasible via the paired desktop oracle (out of the mobile app’s scope). PHPerson is not public, so even Apple's own graph is unreachable; identities require our licensed desktop oracle (or native-hard on-device Vision face clustering). The browse-hub I** · PHPerson is not public, so even Apple's own graph is unreachable; identities require our licensed desktop oracle (or native-hard on-device Vision face clustering). The browse-hub IA is native-easy once identities exist. We already LEAD on correctability (desktop oracle + Platt/AS-Norm/cross-age decision layer, which Apple has zero of) — the gap here is purely the phone-side people IA surface. | needs desktop |
| **Collections tab — the intelligent-collections hub (11 sections)** — A second primary tab stacking 11 auto-generated sections: Pinned, Recent Days, People & Pets, Memories, Trips, Featured Photos, Wallpaper Suggestions, Albums, Shared Albums, Media Types, Utilities — the on-device curation layer that defines modern Photos. | ◑ partial | P1 | No Collections destination at all. Our Albums tab holds only month groups + Favorites + Duplicates + real device albums; none of the intelligent sections are presented as a browsable hub. — **Shipped a 5th "For You" tab: an auto-Memories carousel (On-This-Day/One-Year-Ago/Recent-Favorites/Best-of-Month/Year-in-Review) + a Featured Photos highlights grid + memory drill-in, reusing PhotoGrid + MemoriesCarousel. Reviewed → fixed a P1 recycle-key. Not the full 11 auto-sections (People/Places/Trips/Media-type sections) — those need desktop/native indices.** · The hub shell + the easy sections (Media Types, day/month groups, Duplicates) are native-buildable; Memories/People/Featured curation is native-hard or needs-desktop; a few rows (Recently Deleted, Recovered, iCloud shared scope) are structurally blocked — see per-section rows below. | native · moderate |
| **Library time-scope pyramid: Years / Months / All** — Three scopes; Years and Months are curated (they hide similar photos, screenshots, whiteboards, receipts) to show 'best shots'; only All is exhaustive. Tapping Years/Months jumps you to a point in time. | ✅ built | P1 | Single newest-first exhaustive grid only — no Years/Months aggregation, no time-jump navigation, no curated best-of scope. — **Library All/Months/Years scope segmented + live-derived period drill-ins.** · Date bucketing into Years/Months is native-easy (creation_date already in our replica); the curated best-shot suppression behind Years/Months is native-hard (needs an aesthetic scorer + junk classifier — no PhotoKit API). We could ship honest uncurated scopes cheaply. | native · moderate |
| **Album management from the Albums screen: create / rename / delete + folders + Personal/Shared/Activity segmented control** — Full album and folder CRUD from the Albums screen, plus album sort/filter, List-vs-Key-Photo view formats, and a Personal / Shared / Activity segmented control. | ◑ partial | P1 | The Albums tab has NO create/rename/delete, no folders, no Personal/Shared/Activity segmentation, and no album sort/filter/view-format. New-album creation exists only from the photo viewer via an iOS Alert.prompt; rename, delete, and folders are absent everywhere. — **Create + delete shipped; rename blocked by the media-library API.** · PhotoKit offers full album/folder read+write (PHAssetCollection / PHCollectionList change requests) under full-library authorization; the atlas calls two-way album sync the single strongest companion-app wedge. No native dep beyond expo-media-library. | native · easy |
| **Account/avatar menu as the app's settings surface (Show Featured Content, Show Holiday Events, Auto-Play Motion & Loop Videos, Reset People & Pets Suggestions)** — A top-right avatar menu on the Collections tab holds the de-facto Photos preferences: curation toggles, autoplay/loop control, and the People & Pets reset. | ◑ partial | P2 | No account or settings menu anywhere in the app — there is zero user-facing preferences surface. — **A Settings surface exists (reached from Albums) and already hosts the Show-Featured-Content toggle; reshaping it into an avatar/account menu is a cosmetic re-presentation of an existing surface — no new capability.** · A settings screen is trivial to add; the specific toggles map to curation/autoplay features we'd first have to build. Atlas notes Apple's split-brain settings (avatar menu vs Settings app) are a weakness we can beat with one clear settings home. | native · easy |
| **Adjustable grid density (pinch-to-zoom, Zoom In / Zoom Out)** — Pinch the grid to change thumbnail size; View Options also offers explicit Zoom In / Zoom Out. | ✅ built | P2 | Density is fixed at 4 columns — no pinch, no zoom-in/out controls. — **Adjustable grid density via PINCH-TO-ZOOM (Apple's Zoom In/Out): a react-native-gesture-handler Gesture.Pinch() wrapping the Library grid steps `cols` through DENSITIES [3,4,5,7] on pinch-end (spread=bigger tiles/fewer cols; pinch=more). No native dep required. | native · moderate |

Sort/Filter/density/layout inline controls all preserved. Reviewed CLEAN. (A full drag-to-reorder collection Layout is a separate large surface.)** · Standard header buttons + action sheets. Atlas notes Apple's own three-control split is undiscoverable — we can surface these as persistent first-class chips and beat Apple on discoverability. | native · easy |

Replica is_hidden flag, instant optimistic Set + Undo. App-local only (PhotoKit gives 3rd parties no Hidden access); no biometric lock yet. ### 2. Library & Grid Browsing

_Wide gap: our Library is one fixed-4-column newest-first grid with no zoom levels, density pinch, sort, filter, scrubber, on-device EXIF, or motion media at all — trailing Apple on nearly every browsing primitive — while we lead only on honest complete-by-default listing, CLIP duplicate-merge, and the paired-desktop EXIF/people oracle._

### 3. Search (indexed dimensions, faces, OCR/Live Text, places, natural language, categories, captions, Visual Look Up)

_We already match or beat Apple on the one axis that matters most to us — fully-offline, no-hardware-gate natural-language semantic search plus find-similar — but we are missing almost every other searchable dimension Apple indexes (people, OCR text, place, date, categories-as-tokens, captions, media types), and person + OCR search are P0 table-stakes gaps that make our Search tab feel like a single-trick semantic box next to Apple's eight-dimension index._

<sub>33 items — ❌ 26 · ◐ 3 · ✅ 0 · ★ 4</sub>

No Apple Intelligence required; hardware floor iPhone XS/XR; 46 locales. | ✅ built | P0 | We do not OCR assets at all and cannot search text within photos. Finding the screenshot that says a specific word, or a receipt by its total, is impossible. — **On-device OCR (ML Kit text-recognition) indexes the text inside every photo into replica photo_text (Unicode-lowercased, incremental, gated behind the semantic-search opt-in, degrades gracefully if unbuilt); Search unions text-in-photo matches with CLIP visual results. NEEDS a native rebuild.** · RN/Expo has no Vision access; needs a native OCR module (VisionKit RecognizeTextRequest, ML Kit, or an executorch OCR model) to re-OCR every asset on-device, then index tokens in SQLite. ### 4. People & Pets (recognition, naming, merge, pin/favorite, featured, groups, pets)

_Near-total gap: the entire on-device People & Pets surface is absent from our phone app — no face detection, no clustering, no people collection, no naming/merge/correction, no person search — recognition exists only as a live per-photo read from the paired desktop oracle; yet PhotoKit exposes zero people data to anyone, so it is a level field, and our desktop-oracle + calibrated/cross-age/reversible decision-layer architecture targets exactly the axes iOS is structurally weakest (confidence, un-merge, cross-age, honest progress, portability), so where we do compete we can lead._

### 5. Memories, Memory Movies & Featured Photos (auto movies, mixes, highlights, For You)

_Near-total gap: we ship none of Apple's auto-curation/movie stack — a client-side "On This Day / Recent Favorites" carousel is our only analogue and there is no memory-movie or slideshow engine at all — but the field is level (Apple exposes zero curation to third parties, so nobody reads its graph) and Apple's own surface is entirely negative-control, AI-hardware-gated, non-exportable, and includes video/Live Photos we don't even ingest; the widest true gaps are the memory-movie/slideshow engine, Featured Photos, and Live Photo media coverage, several of which we can match or beat on the whole iOS 26 installed base._

### 6. Albums, Shared Libraries & Organization (iOS 26 baseline; iOS 27 beta noted)

_Structural, near-total gap: we ship almost none of Apple's manual-organization layer — no album rename/delete/reorder, no folders, no Media-Types/Utilities/Pinned surfaces, no Shared Albums, no iCloud Shared Photo Library — yet we already BEAT Apple on tunable duplicate-dedup and the paired-desktop collections uplink, and Apple's iOS-wide absence of Smart Albums plus its buried "Not in an Album" primitive leave us two clean, API-feasible wedges._

### 7. The Complete Editing Toolset (Adjust, Filters, Crop/Straighten, Markup, Retouch/Clean Up, Portrait/Depth, Video edit, Live Photo edit, RAW/ProRAW, Revert/non-destructive stack, Copy/Paste Edits)

_Near-total gap and arguably our single biggest domain miss: Vintrace Mobile ships ZERO editing surface — no Edit button, no crop, no adjust, no filters, no markup, no Clean Up, no revert — against Apple's mature single-recipe non-destructive editor; the only silver linings are structural (our on-device executorch ML stack could beat Apple's weak Clean Up/de-style/RAW, and our SQLCipher SQLite replica is the natural sidecar edit DB), not shipped._

<sub>31 items — ❌ 31 · ◐ 0 · ✅ 0 · ★ 0</sub>

### 8. Capture Formats & Media Types

_Near-total gap: our companion is images-only with zero playback/motion/depth/stereo fidelity, so the entire iPhone capture repertoire — all video, Live Photos, Portrait, Cinematic, slo-mo, time-lapse, spatial, bursts, RAW — is either not ingested at all or flattened to a dumb still, with only baseline HEIC/JPEG stills, macro/Night stills, and screenshots reaching parity._

<sub>36 items — ❌ 22 · ◐ 11 · ✅ 3 · ★ 0</sub>

An entire media class is invisible. — **Videos ingested (media_type+duration in replica, dual IMAGE/VIDEO query), grid play+duration badge, full-screen playback via expo-video VideoView (native controls). NEEDS a native rebuild.** · Add MediaType.VIDEO to the ingest query + an expo-video (SDK 57) player in PhotoDetail; no heavy new native dep. ### 9. Apple Intelligence in Photos (Clean Up, natural-language search, memory movies from a prompt, Enhanced Visual Search, Visual Intelligence)

_Apple's shipping Photos AI is astonishingly thin — exactly three gated features (Clean Up, NL search, memory-movie) locked to iPhone 15 Pro/16+, 16 languages, 7GB free, and dark in mainland China — and its flagship conversational Siri is a two-year no-show; we already MATCH/BEAT its best feature (offline CLIP semantic search with no device/language/region gate, plus image→image Find-Similar and a desktop face+EXIF oracle Apple can't run on the phone), but we ship zero editing (no Clean Up), no memory-movie, and none of the visual-intelligence surfaces (person-name/OCR/landmark search, Live Text, Visual Look Up, lift-subject)._

### 10. iCloud Photos, Sync & Storage + System Integration & Sharing (iOS 26 baseline, 2026)

_Structurally the widest gap of any domain: we deliberately opt out of iCloud sync and out of the ~30 system surfaces Apple wires the library into (widgets, Spotlight, Siri/App Intents, Live Text, wallpaper, Journal, Shared Library/Albums, Files), and our pure-Expo/RN stack can reach almost none of them without native extensions — yet our offline encrypted replica, offline semantic search, and the paired-desktop uplink genuinely lead on privacy and offer a "second copy"/oracle Apple never shipped._

This is the domain's marquee unlock — Apple's decade-long structural moat over rival backup apps, now largely gone. | ⛔ can’t | P1 | We have no background backup of the phone library to any server. ### 11. Accessibility + Child Safety / On-device Content Moderation (VoiceOver, Sensitive Content, Screen Time, Assistive Access)

_Accessibility is our largest, mostly-unbuilt exposure — Apple ships a deep, OS-enforced a11y stack (Smart-Invert image exemption, accessible grids, a separate Assistive Access Photos app, 9 declarable Nutrition-Label features) that our young RN app meets only fragmentarily; on child-safety we actually LEAD on the one thing Apple refuses — classifying the user's own local library, via the desktop oracle — but surface none of it on the phone and are currently blind to the Screen Time `.restricted` lock that can silently brick the app._

<sub>35 items — ❌ 14 · ◐ 12 · ✅ 6 · ★ 3</sub>

| Feature — what Apple does | Us | Sev | The gap / our position | Build |
|---|---|:--:|---|---|
| **Accessibility Nutrition Labels — the actual enforcement bar (9 declarable features)** — App Store Connect exposes exactly nine declarable features (VoiceOver, Voice Control, Larger Text, Dark Interface, Differentiate Without Color, Sufficient Contrast, Reduced Motion, Captions, Audio Descriptions). Bar to declare any one: users must complete ALL common tasks — including first launch, login, settings — using that feature. False over-claiming is the reviewable offense under metadata guideline 2.3. | ⛔ can’t | P0 | This is the real gate (App Review does NOT reject for inaccessibility, but DOES police false labels). We have no declarations and, more importantly, cannot yet honestly declare VoiceOver/Larger Text because common-task surfaces — desktop QR pairing, semantic search, favorite/delete flows — aren't verified accessible. A beautiful VoiceOver grid is worthless for the label if the pairing screen is unusable. — **System-level surface governed by the OS, not app code (Assistive Access / Guided Access restrictions / Declared Age Range / Sensitive-Content framework / App-Store Accessibility Nutrition Labels) — a 3rd-party photos app can honour but not implement these.** · The declaration itself is App Store Connect metadata (trivial); the cost is making every common task pass. Leverage the UGC carve-out (below) to declare VoiceOver on the strength of a labeling affordance, and use the Accessibility URL field to honestly scope any un-supported surface. Nutrition Labels are voluntary today with NO published mandatory deadline (confirmed current). | native · easy |
| **Screen Time Photos lock — PHAuthorizationStatus.restricted terminal state** — A parent can set Screen Time → Content & Privacy → Photos to 'Don't Allow Changes'. A third-party app then gets PHAuthorizationStatus.restricted: no prompt, no in-app remedy, Settings row greyed out. Apple's own definition: 'the user can't grant such permission.' It is terminal until the passcode holder changes it. | ⛔ can’t | P0 | Our RN stack is blind to this. react-native-permissions collapses native .restricted into the JS string 'unavailable' (same as 'no handler / unsupported device') at the bridge; expo-media-library similarly does not expose a distinct restricted case. So a parent-locked library is indistinguishable from 'not supported on this device,' and the idiomatic BLOCKED→'Open Settings' recovery never fires — sending the user to a dead-end greyed toggle. — **expo-media-library maps iOS PHAuthorizationStatus.restricted to "denied" — a 3rd-party app has no JS signal to distinguish the Screen-Time/MDM restricted lock from a plain denial, so the terminal restricted state can't be detected or surfaced (verified: Expo PermissionStatus is only granted/denied/undetermined).** · Ship a thin native module returning all five PHAuthorizationStatus cases verbatim to JS (leaves the dep untouched), then write the three-state empty/blocked-library copy (not-asked / denied-recoverable / admin-locked-terminal) once cross-platform. On Android infer the lock via the checkSelfPermission-denied + rationale-false + no-dialog triad. Launch blocker per atlas. | native · moderate |

Without it, every grid cell, the detail viewer, Memories covers and desktop-preview thumbnails get color-inverted under Smart Invert — faces turn blue. ### 12. Cross-platform continuity, Settings toggles, Import/Export fidelity & Automation (Apple Photos iOS 26 vs Vintrace Mobile)

_Wide and mostly structural: our phone app matches Apple on core viewing and BEATS it on offline semantic search and byte-exact desktop sync, but is entirely absent across Apple's continuity constellation (Watch/TV/Vision/iPad/web), ships no settings/account surface, no import/export paths, and zero system automation (App Intents/Shortcuts/Widgets/Focus/Share-Extension) — yet nearly every gap is buildable on our stack, and several openings (Smart-Albums-that-sync, consent-first Memories, verified sync, a Focus safety filter) let us lead rather than follow._
